12.2.4
, the paths of integration can be taken to be the boundaries of

\rho_{1}<|z_{i}|<\rho_{2}

with the positive orientations; we are assuming that

\rho_{1}<1<\rho_{2},

with
\rho_{2}-\rho_{1}
sufficiently small. ​It follows at once that the average

(5)

\sum_{\epsilon}\frac{R_{1}(q^{-1/2}t_{1}^{\epsilon_{1}},\ldots,q^{-1/2}t_{r}^{\epsilon_{r}})}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}t_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}g}\prod_{1\leq i\leq j\leq r}(1-t_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}t_{j}^{\epsilon_{j}})}

is holomorphic in a neighborhood of
|t_{i}|=1

(1\leq i\leq r).
In particular, the function
\mathcal{Q}_{1}(t;g,q)\psi(t)^{-g}
is continuous on
\mathbf{T}.

12.2.8
.

The following proposition provides an explicit formula for the first term

\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})

in Conjecture
12.1.8
.

12.2.9 Proposition
.

For every partition
\lambda\subseteq(r^{g})
we have

(6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})=\frac{(1-q^{-1})}{r!}\cdot\frac{(-1)^{r(r-1)/\penalty 2}}{\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)^{r}}\!\oint\limits_{|z_{r}|=1}\cdots\oint\limits_{|z_{1}|=1}R_{1}(q^{-1/\penalty 2}\mathrm{z})\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(z_{i}-z_{j})

\displaystyle\cdot\,\mathrm{det}\left(z_{i}^{g-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j}-z_{i}^{-(g-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}\,\frac{d\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{z}^{g+r+1}}.

Proof.

By definition

\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})=\frac{1}{\#W}\!\int_{\mathbf{T}}\mathcal{Q}_{1}(t;g,q)\psi(t)^{-g}\overline{s_{\langle\lambda^{{\dagger}}\rangle}(t)}\,|\delta(t)|^{2}\,dt.

The function

\mathcal{Q}_{1}(t;g,q)\psi(t)^{-g}

is equal to the average (
5
) multiplied by the factor

(1-q^{-1})
.
Using Lemma
12.2.6
, we express (
5
) as an
r
-fold
integral over the boundaries of

\rho_{1}<|z_{i}|<\rho_{2}

with the positive orientations. As above, we take

\rho_{1}<1<\rho_{2},

with

\rho_{2}-\rho_{1}

sufficiently small. This integral can be decomposed as a sum of
2^{r}

r
-fold integrals over circles

|z_{i}|=\rho_{k}

(
k=1,2
). Pick one of them, say when the first
n
integrals are over

|z_{i}|=\rho_{1}

(
i=1,\ldots,n
), while the remaining ones are over

|z_{i}|=\rho_{2}.

To the denominator of the expression under the integral, we apply Cauchy’s identity for

\mathrm{Sp}(2r)
(see §
2.6.8
), which states that

\prod_{i,j=1}^{r}(1-t_{i}x_{\!j})^{-1}(1-t_{i}x_{\!j}^{-1})^{-1}\,=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(1-t_{i}t_{\!j})^{-1}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
l(\mu)\leq r\end{subarray}}s_{\langle\mu\rangle}(x_{1}^{\pm 1}\!,\ldots,x_{r}^{\pm 1})s_{\mu}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{r}).

To ensure the absolute convergence of the series involved, we change
z_{i}
to

z_{i}^{-1}

for
n+1\leq i\leq r,
and apply the simple identity

\frac{1}{(1-z_{i}a_{j})(1-z_{i}a_{j}^{-1})}=\frac{z_{i}^{-2}}{(1-z_{i}^{-1}a_{j})(1-z_{i}^{-1}a_{j}^{-1})}.

Accordingly, if we set

\epsilon_{i}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $1\leq i\leq n$}\\
-1&\text{if $n+1\leq i\leq r$}\end{cases}

then by performing the integral over
\mathbf{T},
using the orthonormality of the symplectic Schur functions,
we can write the contribution to

\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})

as

\begin{split}I_{\epsilon}:=\frac{(1-q^{-1})}{r!}\frac{(-1)^{\frac{r(r+1)}{2}+n}}{\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)^{r}}\oint\limits_{|z_{r}|=\rho_{2}}\cdots\oint\limits_{|z_{1}|=\rho_{1}}R_{1}(q^{-1/\penalty 2}\mathrm{z})&\frac{\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(z_{j}-z_{i})^{2}(1-z_{i}z_{j})}{\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\big(1-z_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}z_{j}^{\epsilon_{j}}\big)}\\
&\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{r}z_{k}^{-r(1-\epsilon_{k})}\cdot s_{\lambda^{\dagger}}(\mathrm{z^{\epsilon}})\,\frac{d\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{z}^{g+r}}\end{split}

where
\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{r}).
Notice that

(-1)^{n}=(-1)^{-r}\prod_{i=1}^{r}\epsilon_{i}.

Using the identities

\frac{(z_{i}-z_{j})(1-z_{i}z_{j})}{1-z_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}z_{j}^{\epsilon_{j}}}=z_{i}^{1-\epsilon_{i}}z_{j}^{1-\epsilon_{j}}\big(z_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}-z_{j}^{\epsilon_{j}}\big)\;\;\;\text{and}\;\;\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}z_{i}^{1-\epsilon_{i}}z_{j}^{1-\epsilon_{j}}=\prod_{k=1}^{r}z_{k}^{(r-1)(1-\epsilon_{k})}

we can simplify
I_{\epsilon}
to:

\begin{split}I_{\epsilon}:=\frac{(1-q^{-1})}{r!}\frac{(-1)^{\frac{r(r-1)}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}\epsilon_{i}}{\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)^{r}}\oint\limits_{|z_{r}|=1}\cdots\oint\limits_{|z_{1}|=1}R_{1}(q^{-1/\penalty 2}\mathrm{z})&\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(z_{i}-z_{j})\big(z_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}-z_{j}^{\epsilon_{j}}\big)\\
&\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{r}z_{k}^{\epsilon_{k}}\cdot s_{\lambda^{\dagger}}(\mathrm{z^{\epsilon}})\,\frac{d\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{z}^{g+r+1}}.\end{split}

Also, note that the product

\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\big(z_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}-z_{j}^{\epsilon_{j}}\big)

cancels the denominator of

s_{\lambda^{\dagger}}(\mathrm{z^{\epsilon}}).

Summing now over all tuples

\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{r})\in\{-1,1\}^{r},

the formula for

\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})

is an immediate consequence of the simple identity

\mathrm{det}\!\left(z_{i}^{g-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j}-z_{i}^{-(g-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}=\;\sum_{\epsilon}\,\prod_{k=1}^{r}\epsilon_{k}z_{k}^{\epsilon_{k}}\,\cdot\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\big(z_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}-z_{j}^{\epsilon_{j}}\big)\cdot s_{\lambda^{\dagger}}(\mathrm{z^{\epsilon}})

which follows from the multilinearity of the determinant.
This completes the proof.
∎

12.2.10
.

As a first corollary, we obtain the expected connection between the stable traces
T_{\lambda}
and

\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}}).

12.2.11 Corollary
.

For any fixed partition

\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\cdots)
with

\lambda_{1}\leq r,

we have

(7)

\begin{split}&\lim_{g\to\infty}\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})=T_{\lambda}=\frac{(1-q^{-1})}{r!}\cdot\frac{(-1)^{r(r-1)/\penalty 2}}{\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)^{r}}\!\oint\limits_{|z_{r}|=1}\cdots\oint\limits_{|z_{1}|=1}R_{1}(q^{-1/\penalty 2}\mathrm{z})\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(z_{i}-z_{j})\\
&\hskip 140.0pt\cdot\,\mathrm{det}\Big(z_{i}^{-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}\,\frac{d\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{z}^{r+1}}.\end{split}

Proof.

In formula (
6
), divide the determinant by
\mathrm{z}^{g}.
Applying the Riemann-Lebesgue lemma, we see at once that the limit and the
r
-fold integral in (
7
) are equal.

To see that the integral is also equal to

T_{\lambda},

divide the determinant by
\mathrm{z}^{r},
and note that

(-1)^{r(r-1)/\penalty 2}\mathrm{det}\Big(z_{i}^{\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+j-1}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}=\;\mathrm{det}\Big(z_{i}^{\lambda_{j}^{\prime}+r-j}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}=\;s_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathrm{z})\,\cdot\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(z_{i}-z_{j}).

Recalling that
R_{1}(\mathrm{z})
is the Schur-generating function of the stable traces
T_{\lambda},
it follows at once from the Peter-Weyl theorem
[
Bum04
]
, applied to the compact group
\mathrm{U}(r),
that the integral in (
7
) can be decomposed as

\frac{(1-q^{-1})}{r!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
l(\mu^{\prime})\leq r\end{subarray}}T_{\mu}\cdot\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)^{r}}\!\oint\limits_{|z_{r}|=1}\cdots\oint\limits_{|z_{1}|=1}s_{\mu^{\prime}}(\mathrm{z})\overline{s_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathrm{z})}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}|z_{i}-z_{j}|^{2}\,\frac{d\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{z}}.

Letting

z_{i}=e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\theta_{i}},

0\leq\theta_{i}\leq 1,

and using the orthonormality of the Schur functions, one finds that the last
integral is
1
or
0
according as
\mu=\lambda
or not,
which completes the proof.
∎

12.2.12
.

From the integral representation (
7
) of
T_{\lambda},
we deduce the following estimate:

12.2.13 Corollary
.

For any partition
\lambda
with

\lambda_{1}=l(\lambda^{\prime})\leq r,

we have the estimate

|T_{\lambda}|\ll_{r,\,\varepsilon}q^{\left(-\frac{1}{4}+\varepsilon\right)|\lambda|}.

Proof.

By Lemma
12.2.4
, we can integrate in
(
7
) over

|z_{i}|=q^{\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon}

(instead of
|z_{i}|=1
), for

i=1,\ldots,r,
and after the simple change of variables

z_{i}\to q^{\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon}z_{i},

we can write

\begin{split}T_{\lambda}=\frac{(1-q^{-1})q^{\left(-\frac{1}{4}+\varepsilon\right)|\lambda|}}{r!}\cdot\frac{(-1)^{r(r-1)/\penalty 2}}{\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)^{r}}\!\oint\limits_{|z_{r}|=1}\cdots\oint\limits_{|z_{1}|=1}&R_{1}\big(q^{-\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon}\mathrm{z}\big)\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(z_{i}-z_{j})\\
&\cdot\,\mathrm{det}\Big(z_{i}^{-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}\,\frac{d\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{z}^{r+1}}.\end{split}

Applying the upper bound in Lemma
12.2.4
, and the Hadamard inequality for determinants, we have

\left|R_{1}\big(q^{-\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon}\mathrm{z}\big)\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(z_{i}-z_{j})\cdot\,\mathrm{det}\Big(z_{i}^{-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}\right|<r^{r}\big(1-q^{-4\varepsilon}\big)^{-2^{1+r(r+1)/\penalty 2}}

and our assertion follows.
∎

12.2.14 Remark
.

This estimate can also be seen from
7.0.2
and
11.3.15
, although upon sufficient unwinding the proofs are not so different. Indeed, if
|\lambda|=w
and
\lambda_{1}\leq r
then
T_{\lambda}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{|\lambda|=w}|T_{\lambda}|\dim S^{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathbf{C}^{r})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{|\lambda|=w}\sum_{k\geq w/4}q^{-k}\dim H_{k}(\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{1,0},\mathbb{V}_{\lambda})\dim S^{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathbf{C}^{r})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\geq w/4}q^{-k}2^{\varepsilon k}

for any
\varepsilon>0
. Similarly one may show, using
7.0.12
instead of
7.0.2
, that
|T_{\lambda}|\ll_{r,\varepsilon}q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)\ell(\lambda)}
.

12.2.15 Remark
.

Notice that, by Remark
12.2.5
, one can use the holomorphy of

R_{1}(\mathrm{t})

in a larger polydisc to improve the estimate in Corollary
12.2.13
, and hence the range of
\lambda
for which
H^{k}(\mathcal{H}_{g}^{1,0},\mathbb{V}_{\lambda})=0
for
k<\frac{g}{2}.
For instance, using the holomorphy of

R_{1}(q^{-1/\penalty 2}\mathrm{z})

on the polydisc

|z_{i}|\leq q^{\frac{1}{3}-\varepsilon},

i=1,\ldots,r,
one can show as in the proof of Corollary
12.2.13
that

|T_{\lambda}|\ll_{r,\,\varepsilon}q^{\left(-\frac{1}{3}+\varepsilon\right)|\lambda|+\frac{r^{2}(r^{2}-1)}{36}}

and the corresponding bound on
|\lambda|
is obtained by imposing the
condition

q^{\left(-\frac{1}{3}+\varepsilon\right)|\lambda|+r^{2}(r^{2}-1)/{36}}<q^{-{g}/{2}}

which, upon replacing
\varepsilon
by
\varepsilon/\penalty 4,
is implied by

|\lambda|>(1+\varepsilon)\!\left(3g/\penalty 2+r^{2}(r^{2}-1)/\penalty 12\right)
.

12.2.16 Corollary
.

For any partition

\lambda
with

\lambda_{1}=l(\lambda^{\prime})\leq r,

we have the estimate

|\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})-T_{\lambda}|\ll_{r,\,\varepsilon}q^{\left(-\frac{1}{4}+\varepsilon\right)|\lambda|-\left(\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon\right)\left(g-\lambda_{1}^{\prime}+1\right)}.

Proof.

For a partition

\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{r}\geq 0),

we set for notational convenience

a_{\mu}(\mathrm{z})=\mathrm{det}\Big(z_{i}^{\mu_{j}+r+1-j}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}.

Then, by the determinant identity in the end of the proof of
12.2.9
we have that

\left|\,\det\left(z_{i}^{g-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j}-z_{i}^{-(g-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}-\;a_{\lambda^{{\dagger}}}(\mathrm{z})\,\right|\,\leq\,\sum_{\epsilon}\,|a_{\lambda^{{\dagger}}}(\mathrm{z^{\epsilon}})|

the sum being over all tuples

\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{r})\in\{-1,1\}^{r}

with at least one component
\epsilon_{i}=-1.

Assuming with no loss of generality that
\epsilon_{1}=-1,
we can estimate

|a_{\lambda^{{\dagger}}}(\mathrm{z^{\epsilon}})|,

with

|z_{i}|=q^{\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon},

by applying the Laplace expansion along the first row of

a_{\lambda^{{\dagger}}}(\mathrm{z^{\epsilon}}).

Notice that

\big|z_{1}^{-(g-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j)}\big|\leq q^{-\left(\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon\right)(g-\lambda_{1}^{\prime}+1)}

for all
j,
while by Hadamard’s inequality, the corresponding cofactors are all

\ll_{r}q^{\left(\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon\right)\left((r-1)g-|\lambda|+\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-1+r(r+1)/\penalty 2\right)}.

Thus we get the estimate

\sum_{\epsilon}\,|a_{\lambda^{{\dagger}}}(\mathrm{z^{\epsilon}})|\,\ll_{r}\,q^{\left(\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon\right)\left((r-2)g-|\lambda|+2\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-2+r(r+1)/\penalty 2\right)}.

Now consider the difference between the integrals (
6
) and (
7
). Again, by Lemma
12.2.4
, we can integrate over

|z_{i}|=q^{\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon}\!,

and

|R_{1}(q^{-1/\penalty 2}\mathrm{z})|<\big(1-q^{-4\varepsilon}\big)^{-2^{1+r(r+1)/\penalty 2}}

​on these circles. The remaining piece under the integral is easily seen to be

\ll_{r}q^{\frac{|\lambda|}{2}-\left(\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon\right)\left(rg+\frac{r(r+1)}{2}\right)},

and the estimate in the corollary follows.
∎

12.2.17
.

The estimates in the last two corollaries imply that

|\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})|\ll_{r,\,\varepsilon}q^{-\left(\frac{1}{4}-\varepsilon\right)|\lambda|}

for any partition

\lambda
with

\lambda_{1}=l(\lambda^{\prime})\leq r.

This, combined with Conjecture
12.1.8
, suggests that
the traces

\operatorname{tr}_{\lambda}(g)

indeed decrease as the weight of
\lambda
grows with
g
. Again, the estimate in Corollary
12.2.16
(and hence also this one) can be improved to

|\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})-T_{\lambda}|\ll_{r,\,\varepsilon}q^{\left(-\frac{1}{3}+\varepsilon\right)|\lambda|-\left(\frac{2}{3}-2\varepsilon\right)\left(g-\lambda_{1}^{\prime}+1\right)+\frac{r^{2}(r^{2}-1)}{36}}.

12.3.
The second term

12.3.1
.

To analyze the second term

\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})

in the asymptotic formula of
\operatorname{tr}_{\lambda}(g),
one first checks that the set of roots
\Phi_{2}
is

\Phi_{2}=\Bigg\{\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq j_{1},\,j_{2},\,j_{3}\end{subarray}}^{r}\!\!\!k_{j}\alpha_{j}+\alpha_{j_{1}}\!+\alpha_{j_{2}}\!+\alpha_{j_{3}}\!+2\alpha_{r+1}:\text{$1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}<j_{3}\leq r$, and
$k_{j}\in\{0,2\}$
for
$j\neq\!j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}$}\Bigg\}

see
[
DT23
]
. As in the case of

\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}}),

we shall represent
\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t;g,q)\psi(t)^{-g}
as an integral.
The analogue of the function

R_{1}(\mathrm{t})

is defined as follows. For
\zeta
a
4
th root of
1,
define

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}_{1}=\frac{\left(1-\frac{\xi_{1}}{\zeta q^{3/\penalty 4}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}\right)\!\left(1-\frac{\xi_{2}}{\zeta q^{3/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}\xi_{3}}\right)\!\left(1-\frac{\xi_{3}}{\zeta q^{3/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}\right)}{\left(1-\frac{\zeta\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}{q^{1/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}}\right)\!\left(1-\frac{\zeta\xi_{1}\xi_{3}}{q^{1/\penalty 4}\xi_{2}}\right)\!\left(1-\frac{\zeta\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}{q^{1/\penalty 4}\xi_{3}}\right)},\;\;\;\mathrm{d}_{2}=\frac{\zeta}{q^{3/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}\\
&\mathrm{d}_{3}=\frac{\left(1+\frac{\xi_{1}}{\zeta q^{3/\penalty 4}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}\right)\!\left(1+\frac{\xi_{2}}{\zeta q^{3/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}\xi_{3}}\right)\!\left(1+\frac{\xi_{3}}{\zeta q^{3/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}\right)}{\left(1+\frac{\zeta\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}{q^{1/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}}\right)\!\left(1+\frac{\zeta\xi_{1}\xi_{3}}{q^{1/\penalty 4}\xi_{2}}\right)\!\left(1+\frac{\zeta\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}{q^{1/\penalty 4}\xi_{3}}\right)}\end{split}

and

\mathrm{e}_{1}=\frac{1-(\zeta q^{3/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3})^{-1}}{1-\zeta q^{\,-1/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}},\;\;\;\mathrm{e}_{2}=(\zeta q^{1/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3})^{-1},\;\;\,\text{and}\;\;\;\,\mathrm{e}_{3}=\frac{1+(\zeta q^{3/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3})^{-1}}{1+\zeta q^{\,-1/\penalty 4}\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}.

We set

\begin{split}L_{1}&=\,\tfrac{1}{4}\mathrm{e}_{1}(\mathrm{d}_{1}\!+\mathrm{d}_{3}\!+2\zeta^{2}\mathrm{d}_{2})\,-\,\tfrac{1}{4}\mathrm{e}_{3}(\mathrm{d}_{1}\!+\mathrm{d}_{3}\!-2\zeta^{2}\mathrm{d}_{2})\\
L_{2}&=\,\tfrac{1}{8}\mathrm{e}_{1}(\mathrm{d}_{1}\!+\mathrm{d}_{3}\!+2\zeta^{2}\mathrm{d}_{2})\,+\,\tfrac{1}{8}\mathrm{e}_{3}(\mathrm{d}_{1}\!+\mathrm{d}_{3}\!-2\zeta^{2}\mathrm{d}_{2})\,+\,\tfrac{1}{4}\mathrm{e}_{2}(\mathrm{d}_{1}\!-\mathrm{d}_{3})\\
L_{3}&=\,\tfrac{1}{8}\mathrm{e}_{1}(\mathrm{d}_{1}\!+\mathrm{d}_{3}\!+2\zeta^{2}\mathrm{d}_{2})\,+\,\tfrac{1}{8}\mathrm{e}_{3}(\mathrm{d}_{1}\!+\mathrm{d}_{3}\!-2\zeta^{2}\mathrm{d}_{2})\,-\,\tfrac{1}{4}\mathrm{e}_{2}(\mathrm{d}_{1}\!-\mathrm{d}_{3})\end{split}

and let

S=S(\xi_{1}^{2},\ldots,\xi_{r}^{2},\zeta^{2},q^{-1/2})

be defined by

\begin{split}S=&\,\big(1-q^{-1}\big)\!\left\{\frac{L_{1}}{q^{3/\penalty 4}\zeta\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}+\frac{L_{2}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}(1-q^{-1/\penalty 2}\xi_{i}^{2})}+\frac{L_{3}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}(1+q^{-1/\penalty 2}\xi_{i}^{2})}\right\}\\
&\cdot\,\left(1-\frac{\zeta^{2}\xi_{1}^{2}\xi_{2}^{2}\xi_{3}^{2}}{\sqrt{q}}\right)\prod_{1\leq i\leq 3}\left(1-\frac{\zeta^{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}{\sqrt{q}}\right)\!\left(1-\frac{\zeta^{2}\xi_{1}^{2}\xi_{2}^{2}\xi_{3}^{2}}{\sqrt{q}\,\xi_{i}^{4}}\right)\\
&\!\cdot\;\prod_{i=1}^{3}\prod_{j=4}^{r}\left(1-\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}\xi_{j}^{2}}{q}\right)\!\left(1-\frac{\xi_{i}^{-2}\xi_{j}^{2}}{q}\right)\prod_{4\leq k\leq l\leq r}\left(1-\frac{\xi_{k}^{2}\xi_{l}^{2}}{q}\right)\!.\end{split}

This function is symmetric in the variables

\xi_{1}^{\pm 1},\xi_{2}^{\pm 1},\xi_{3}^{\pm 1},

and (separately) in
\xi_{k}
for
4\leq k\leq r,
see

[
DT23
, Section 7]
. As the above notation suggests, it is also even in
\xi_{i}
(
1\leq i\leq r
) and
\zeta,
and as a function of

q,
it is a polynomial in
q^{-1/\penalty 2}
of the form

S=1+O\Big(q^{-\frac{3}{2}}\Big).

Substitute

t_{i}=\xi_{i}^{2},

and denote the resulting function by

S(\mathrm{t},\zeta^{2}\!,q^{-1/\penalty 2}).

We define
R_{2}(\mathrm{t},\zeta)
by

R_{2}(\mathrm{t},\zeta)=\prod_{n\geq 1}S(\mathrm{t}^{n}\!,\zeta^{2n}\!,q^{-n/\penalty 2})^{i_{n}\!(q)}

the product being absolutely and uniformly convergent for

t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}

in a small neighborhood of the unit circle, and

|t_{k}|\leq q^{\varepsilon}

(
k=4,\ldots,r
) for sufficiently small positive
\varepsilon.
Letting

G(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},\zeta)=-\,\frac{1+\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\zeta^{2}}{\sqrt{q}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left(t_{j}+t_{j}^{-1}\right)}{\left(1-\frac{\zeta^{2}t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}{\sqrt{q}}\right)\!\left(1-\frac{\zeta^{2}}{\sqrt{q}\,t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{3}\left(1-\frac{\zeta^{2}t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}{\sqrt{q}\,t_{i}^{2}}\right)\!\left(1-\frac{\zeta^{2}t_{i}^{2}}{\sqrt{q}\,t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}\right)}

it follows from the computations in
[
DT23
, Section 7]
that

\begin{split}&\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t;g,q)\psi(t)^{-g}=\frac{(1-q^{-1})q^{-g/2-2}}{2^{4}3!(r-3)!}\\
&\cdot\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{r}}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta=1,\sqrt{-1}\\
\epsilon_{\sigma(i)}=\pm 1\end{subarray}}\,\frac{\zeta^{2g}G\!\left(t_{\sigma(1)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(1)}},t_{\sigma(2)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(2)}},t_{\sigma(3)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(3)}},\zeta\right)\!R_{2}\!\left(t_{\sigma(1)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(1)}},\ldots,t_{\sigma(r)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(r)}},\zeta\right)\left(t_{\sigma(4)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(4)}}\cdots\,t_{\sigma(r)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(r)}}\right)^{-g}}{\prod_{i=1}^{3}\prod_{j=4}^{r}\left(1-t_{\sigma(i)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(i)}}t_{\sigma(j)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(j)}}\right)\left(1-t_{\sigma(i)}^{-\epsilon_{\sigma(i)}}t_{\sigma(j)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(j)}}\right)\,\cdot\prod_{4\leq k\leq l\leq r}\left(1-t_{\sigma(k)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(k)}}t_{\sigma(l)}^{\epsilon_{\sigma(l)}}\right)}.\end{split}

As before, using the following slight generalization of Lemma
12.2.6
, we can represent
\mathcal{Q}_{2}(t;g,q)\psi(t)^{-g}
as an integral.

12.3.2 Lemma
.

Let

a_{1},\ldots,a_{r}

be distinct non-zero complex numbers such that
a_{i}a_{j}\neq 1
for all

1\leq i,j\leq r.
Suppose
h
is a function of
r
complex variables, holomorphic on a domain containing

\left(a_{\sigma(1)}^{\epsilon_{1}},\ldots,a_{\sigma(r)}^{\epsilon_{r}}\right)

for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{r}
and

\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{r})\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}.

For
0\leq m<r,
define
K_{m}(\mathrm{z})
by

K_{m}(\mathrm{z})=\frac{h(\mathrm{z})}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}\prod_{l=m+1}^{r}(1-z_{k}z_{l})(1-z_{k}^{-1}z_{l})\prod_{m+1\leq k\leq l\leq r}(1-z_{k}z_{l})},\quad\mathrm{z}:=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{r}).

Then we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{r}}\,\sum_{\epsilon}K_{m}\left(a_{\sigma(1)}^{\epsilon_{1}},\ldots,a_{\sigma(r)}^{\epsilon_{r}}\right)\\
&=\frac{(-1)^{r(r+1)/\penalty 2}}{\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)^{r}}\!\oint\cdots\oint h(\mathrm{z})\frac{\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(z_{i}-z_{j})^{\mathrm{e}_{ij}}(1-z_{i}z_{j})\cdot\prod_{1\leq k\leq l\leq m}(1-z_{k}z_{l})\prod_{i=1}^{m}z_{i}^{r-m}}{\prod_{i,j=1}^{r}(1-z_{i}a_{j})(1-z_{i}a_{j}^{-1})}\frac{d\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{z}^{r}}\end{split}

where
\mathrm{e}_{ij}=1
or
2
according as

i\leq m
and
j\geq m+1
or not,

\mathrm{z}^{r}:=z_{1}^{r}\cdots\,z_{r}^{r},

and where each path of integration encloses the

a_{j}^{\pm 1},
but not

z_{j}\!=0.

Proof.

The same idea as in the proof of
[
DT23
, Lemma 7.1]
. Alternatively, the lemma can be easily deduced from
[
DT23
, Lemma 7.5]
.
∎

12.3.3
.

We apply this lemma with
m=3
and

h(z_{1},\ldots,z_{r})\;=\sum_{\zeta=1,\sqrt{-1}}\,\zeta^{2g}G(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},\zeta)R_{2}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{r},\zeta)(z_{4}\,\cdots\,z_{r})^{-g}.

Following the same idea as in the proof of Proposition
12.2.9
, one obtains:

12.3.4 Proposition
.

For every partition
\lambda\subseteq(r^{g})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})=\frac{(1-q^{-1})q^{-g/2-2}}{2^{4}3!(r-3)!}\cdot\frac{(-1)^{r(r-1)/\penalty 2}}{\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)^{r}}\!\oint\limits_{|z_{r}|=1}\cdots\oint\limits_{|z_{1}|=1}h(\mathrm{z})\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq r}(z_{i}-z_{j})^{\mathrm{e}_{ij}-1}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{1\leq k\leq l\leq 3}(1-z_{k}z_{l})\,\prod_{i=1}^{3}z_{i}^{r-3}\cdot\,\det\left(z_{i}^{g-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j}-z_{i}^{-(g-\lambda_{r+1-j}^{\prime}+r+1-j)}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}\,\frac{d\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{z}^{r+1}}.

12.3.5
.

For large
g,
the value of

|\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})|

is as large as possible for partitions
\lambda
such that

g-\lambda_{i}^{\prime}=O(1)

for
i=1
,
2,3
, and

\lambda_{i}^{\prime}=O(1)

for
i\geq 4.
For instance, take
r=3
and

\lambda^{\prime}=(g,g,g)

with
g
even, that is,
\lambda=(3,3,3,\ldots)
. In this case,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})=-\,\frac{(1-q^{-1})q^{-g/2-2}}{2^{4}3!}\cdot\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\right)^{3}}\!\oint\limits_{|z_{3}|=1}\,\oint\limits_{|z_{2}|=1}\,\oint\limits_{|z_{1}|=1}h(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})

\displaystyle\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}(z_{i}-z_{j})\,\cdot\prod_{1\leq k\leq l\leq 3}(1-z_{k}z_{l})\cdot\,\mathrm{det}\!\left(z_{i}^{4-j}-z_{i}^{-4+j}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 3}\,\frac{dz_{1}dz_{2}dz_{3}}{z_{1}^{4}z_{2}^{4}z_{3}^{4}}

with

h(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})\;=\sum_{\zeta=1,\sqrt{-1}}G(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},\zeta)R_{2}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},\zeta).

We have

\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})=-\,(1-q^{-1})q^{-g/2-2}\left(1-q^{-2}+2q^{-3}-9q^{-4}-32q^{-5}+\cdots\right)

which can be easily checked as follows. The function

S(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},\zeta^{2}\!,x)

is a polynomial in
x
of degree
14
of the form

S(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},\zeta^{2}\!,x)=1+O\!\left(x^{3}\right)\!,

and thus

R_{2}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},\zeta)\,=\prod_{n\leq 10}S(z_{1}^{n},z_{2}^{n},z_{3}^{n},\zeta^{2n}\!,q^{-n/\penalty 2})^{i_{n}\!(q)}\,\cdot\,\left(1+O\!\left(q^{-\frac{11}{2}}\right)\right)\!.

Use this approximation, and truncate the power series expansion of

G(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},\zeta)

to its
q^{-5}
th term. Replacing
G
and
R_{2}
under the integral of

\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})

by their approximations and truncating again at the
q^{-5}
th term,
our assertion follows by direct computation.

12.3.6
.

On the other hand, §
12.2.17
implies that

\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})\ll_{r,\,\varepsilon}q^{\left(-\frac{\rho}{4}+\varepsilon\right)g}

when
(|\lambda|/\penalty g)\geq\rho,
for a positive
\rho
. In particular, if
\lambda_{i}=3
for all
i,
then

\operatorname{tr}_{\lambda}(g)\sim\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})

when
g\to\infty,
which shows that

\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})

dominates

\mathcal{T}_{1}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})

in this case. In fact
\operatorname{tr}_{\lambda}(g)\sim\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})
can be proven unconditionally,
since the asymptotic formula (
4
) when
r=3
with an error term on the order of

q^{\left(-\frac{2}{3}+\varepsilon\right)g}
can be established by a similar technique to that used in
[
Dia19
]
for the cubic moment of central values of quadratic
L
-functions.

12.3.7
.

The regular behavior of the second term

\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda^{{\dagger}})

when

g-\lambda_{i}^{\prime}=O(1)

for
i=1,2,3
and

\lambda_{i}^{\prime}=O(1)

for
i\geq 4
suggests an explanation in terms of
secondary stability
in the sense of Kupers–Galatius–Randal-Williams
[
GKRW19
]
for the coefficients

\mathbb{V}_{\lambda}
. It will be interesting to further investigate this phenomenon in the different regimes of
\lambda,
dictated by significant changes in the behavior of the traces
\operatorname{tr}_{\lambda}(g).
